  [MEDIUM] IssueType.RUNTIME -- Line 27
  window.setTimeout assumes browser environment. In Next.js 'use client' components, window is available after hydration, but during SSR or if this component is somehow rendered on the server (e.g., via a bug or misconfiguration), window.setTimeout will throw a ReferenceError. While 'use client' prevents SSR, this is a brittle pattern.
  Fix: Use a safe reference: const safeSetTimeout = typeof window !== 'undefined' ? window.setTimeout : setTimeout; or use the global setTimeout which is available in both environments.

  [LOW] IssueType.EDGE_CASE -- Line 23

For example, 'abc' <= 0 is false, so visible will be false and the timeout will be set with a string value, which setTimeout will coerce to 0, causing immediate rendering.
  Fix: Add a guard to coerce delayMs to a number explicitly: const delay = Number(delayMs); const [visible, setVisible] = useState(delay <= 0);
  Code: const [visible, setVisible] = useState(delayMs <= 0);

  [LOW] IssueType.EDGE_CASE -- Line 27

  Fix: Consider clamping delayMs to a minimum of 0 or documenting that negative values are treated as 0.
  Code: const id = window.setTimeout(() => setVisible(true), delayMs);

  FILE: TagPicker.jsx
  Score: 95/100   Issues: 3   Provider: nvidia   Time: 319.51s

  TagPicker is a clean, well-structured controlled component with good accessibility and edge-case handling. The primary risk is the use of `tag` as the React key, which will cause rendering bugs if duplicate tags are ever added (the dedup check is case-sensitive and uses `includes`). Additionally, the component silently ignores duplicate tags without user feedback, which may confuse users. The component is otherwise production-ready with proper disabled state handling, keyboard navigation, and blur-based submission.

  [MEDIUM] IssueType.BUG -- Line 58
  Using `tag` as the React key is unsafe because the dedup check on line 17 uses `tags.includes(trimmed)` which is case-sensitive. If a user adds 'React' and then 'react', both will be added as separate tags but the key will be the same string, causing React to reuse the DOM node incorrectly. More critically, if the parent component ever allows duplicate tags (e.g., via initial state or external changes), React will log a duplicate key warning and rendering will be unpredictable.
  Fix: Use a unique index or generated ID as the key instead of the tag value itself.
  Code:       {tags.map((tag) => (
        <span
          key={tag}

  Fix: Provide visual feedback for duplicate attempts, such as a brief shake animation, a toast message, or temporarily showing the tag with reduced opacity.
  Code:     if (trimmed && !tags.includes(trimmed)) {
      onChange([...tags, trimmed]);
    }
    setInput('');

  [LOW] IssueType.EDGE_CASE -- Line 30

  FILE: OnboardingChecklist.jsx
  Score: 64/100   Issues: 20   Provider: nvidia   Time: 4674.15s

  The OnboardingChecklist component is well-structured with clear separation of concerns, but it has some minor observability gaps, including silent failure on API errors and lack of error handling for non-2xx responses. The code handles loading and error states, but this approach can mask issues that should be observable. There are also some accessibility concerns, such as missing `aria-label` or `aria-current` attributes, and the use of inline styles and hardcoded pixel values. Overall, the component is production-ready, but it requires some improvements to address the identified issues, with a total of 1 high, 11 medium, and 8 low-severity issues found during the review. The component's functionality and performance can be further ensured by reviewing the entire file and addressing potential problems with data flow, error handling, and state management.

  [HIGH] IssueType.ERROR_HANDLING -- Line 22
  fetcher function does not handle non-2xx HTTP responses. fetch() only rejects on network errors, not HTTP error statuses. If the API returns 404 or 500, res.json() will still parse the body (which may be HTML or error JSON), and SWR will treat it as a successful response with potentially malformed data. This can cause silent failures where data.progress is undefined but error is null.
  Fix: Add response.ok check in fetcher to throw on non-2xx responses, allowing SWR to properly set the error state.
  Code: const fetcher = (url) => fetch(url).then((res) => res.json());

  [MEDIUM] IssueType.OBSERVABILITY -- Line 49
  When error is truthy, the component returns null without logging the error. This silently swallows API failures, making it impossible to debug issues in production. Users see nothing, and operators have no trace of the failure.
  Fix: Log the error to console.error or a monitoring service before returning null, so failures are observable.
  Code: if (isLoading || error || !data?.progress) return null;

  [MEDIUM] IssueType.EDGE_CASE -- Line 55
  If steps array is empty (totalCount = 0), Math.round((0 / 0) * 100) produces NaN. This will be rendered as 'NaN%' in the progress bar width, causing a CSS width of 'NaN%' which is invalid and will be ignored by the browser, resulting in a 0-width bar. While the component returns null if data.progress is missing, an empty steps array is a valid edge case that should be handled.
  Fix: Guard against empty steps array before computing progressPercent.
  Code: const totalCount = steps.length;
const progressPercent = Math.round((completedCount / totalCount) * 100);

  [LOW] IssueType.ERROR_HANDLING -- Line 44
  sessionStorage.getItem() can throw a SecurityError in environments where storage is disabled (e.g., some private browsing modes, iframe sandboxing). This would crash the component and potentially the entire page.
  Fix: Wrap sessionStorage access in a try-catch block to handle environments where storage is unavailable.
  Code: const isDismissed = sessionStorage.getItem(`onboarding_dismissed_${userId}`);

  [LOW] IssueType.ERROR_HANDLING -- Line 61
  sessionStorage.setItem() can also throw a SecurityError or QuotaExceededError. If it fails, the dismiss state won't persist, but the component will still show as dismissed in-memory. This is a minor inconsistency.
  Fix: Wrap sessionStorage.setItem in a try-catch to prevent unhandled exceptions.
  Code: if (userId) sessionStorage.setItem(`onboarding_dismissed_${userId}`, 'true');

  [MEDIUM] IssueType.EDGE_CASE -- Line 192
  If step.href is undefined or null, the Link component will navigate to '/undefined' or '/null', which is likely a broken page. This could happen if the API returns incomplete step data.
  Fix: Add a fallback href or skip rendering the Link if href is missing.
  Code: href={step.href}

  [LOW] IssueType.PERFORMANCE -- Line 204
  onMouseEnter handler directly mutates the DOM element's style on every hover event. This bypasses React's rendering pipeline and could cause layout thrashing if many items are rendered. CSS :hover pseudo-class would be more performant and maintainable.
  Fix: Replace inline onMouseEnter/onMouseLeave with CSS :hover selector on the Link component.
  Code: onMouseEnter={(e) => {
  if (!isNext) e.currentTarget.style.background = 'var(--bg-secondary)';
}}

  Fix: Use a small delay before revoking the URL, or use a download approach that doesn't require blob URL revocation timing issues.

  [MEDIUM] IssueType.DATA_INTEGRITY -- Line 23

  Fix: Use a proper unique ID generator like crypto.randomUUID() or a counter-based approach.

  The AssessmentSpreadsheetUploadPanel component has several issues, including a lack of timeout handling and error boundary integration in its fetcher, which could cause hanging requests and unhandled promise rejections in production.   The ScreenSearchBar component has a critical null-pointer risk where `session?.user?.role` is accessed without a null check, causing crashes when session data is loading. The AI search feature lacks error handling, timeout, rate limiting, and debouncing, making it fragile in production. Additionally, the empty-state message exposes the internal environment variable `OPENAI_API_KEY` to end users, creating a minor information disclosure and poor UX. The component is structurally sound but missing essential production hardening for error states, loading conditions, and accessibility.
